#### Acropora vanderhorsti, new species.

--- SENTENCE 2952 ---
Also Mayor's "Growth-Rate of Samoan Corals," plate 9, figs.

--- SENTENCE 2953 ---
24 a, b.

Corallum subarborescent, laxly branching from small incrusting base.

--- SENTENCE 2954 ---
The main branches are 16 to 22 cm.

--- SENTENCE 2955 ---
long and 2 mm. in diameter at their base.

--- SENTENCE 2956 ---
The axial corallites are cylindrical, 2.5 to 3 mm. in diameter, and 3 to 4 mm. exsert.

--- SENTENCE 2957 ---
The wall is very porous, with distinct costæ.

--- SENTENCE 2958 ---
The fossa is 0.85 mm.

--- SENTENCE 2959 ---
There are two cycles of septa.

--- SENTENCE 2960 ---
The directives usually meet and the other members of the first cycle nearly reach to their point of fusion.

--- SENTENCE 2961 ---
The second

cycle is less developed.

--- SENTENCE 2962 ---
The radial corallites are very unequal, the longest ones occurring near the distal portions of the branchlets.

--- SENTENCE 2963 ---
The largest are tubular and stand at an angle of about 85° to 45° with the stem.

--- SENTENCE 2964 ---
They are often curved back slightly, and appear to be a little broader at their top than at their base.

--- SENTENCE 2965 ---
The outer wall is longer than the inner.

--- SENTENCE 2966 ---
The apertures are suboblique; the inner wall is much thinner than the outer and generally is absent for one-third of the length of the corallite.

--- SENTENCE 2967 ---
The septa are 12 in number, but all are rather poorly developed, with the exception of the two directives.

--- SENTENCE 2968 ---
The wall is porous and distinctly costulate with small echinulations.

--- SENTENCE 2969 ---
Between the longest corallites are many smaller ones of the same pattern and different lengths.

--- SENTENCE 2970 ---
Farther down the stem the radial corallites are shorter, and more frequently do subimmersed and even completely immersed corallites appear.

--- SENTENCE 2971 ---
The cœnenchyma is very porous, reticulate, and echinulate.

--- SENTENCE 2972 ---
Costæ of corallite

walls do not reach out into cœnenchyma.

--- SENTENCE 2973 ---
This coral is somewhat like A. listeri (Brook), but differs mainly in its decidedly costulate, radial corallite walls and more porous coenenchyma.

--- SENTENCE 2974 ---
In this latter respect it is very similar to A. nobilis (Dana).

--- SENTENCE 2975 ---
Station.—Pago Pago Harbor, Tutuila, Samoa.

--- SENTENCE 2976 ---
From about 700 feet from shore on Aua Reef flat.

--- SENTENCE 2977 ---
Agitated water, bottom rocky, with broken limestone, about I foot deep at low tide.

#### Acropora pagoensis, new species.

--- SENTENCE 3017 ---
The specimen consists of an elongate, prostrate, and flattened branch with a few scattered, variously sized, suberect branchlets on the upper surface.

--- SENTENCE 3018 ---
The tallest of the branchlets is 2 cm.

--- SENTENCE 3019 ---
in height and 8 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3020 ---
in diameter at the base.

--- SENTENCE 3021 ---
Between the branchlets the comparatively wide expanse of stem is covered with

closely placed radial corallites which are suberect or bending slightly distally.

--- SENTENCE 3022 ---
The under surface possesses a comparatively few scattered, subimmersed corallites with naked coenenchyma between them.

--- SENTENCE 3023 ---
The axial corallites are of the same size as the large radial ones, about 2.5 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3024 ---
in diameter and 2 mm. exsert.

--- SENTENCE 3025 ---
There are two cycles of well-developed septa, the primaries broader than the secondaries, and the two directives meeting in the center of the calice.

--- SENTENCE 3026 ---
Although the wall is rather spongy, it has the appearance of stability.

--- SENTENCE 3027 ---
Outer surface of walls costulate.

--- SENTENCE 3028 ---
The radial corallites are generally arranged in longitudinal rows on the branchlets.

--- SENTENCE 3029 ---
Between the branchlets the upper surface of the branch is taken up by crowded radial corallites of fairly equal size.

--- SENTENCE 3030 ---
Occasionally there appears a longer, proliferous corallite 4 to 6 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3031 ---
in height and 2.5 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3032 ---
Also a few smaller subimmersed or immersed ones can be seen.

--- SENTENCE 3033 ---
The average-sized calice measures 2.5 mm. across and is 2 to 3 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3034 ---
The wall is substantial, the margin of the lower lip rather sharp and at times bent back slightly.

--- SENTENCE 3035 ---
The apertures are large and open.

--- SENTENCE 3036 ---
The lower lip is longer than the upper; corallites are tubular or may be slightly compressed to become tubo-nariform or gutter-shaped.

--- SENTENCE 3037 ---
The septa are remarkably well developed; 6 broad primaries alternate with narrower intermediates; the directives usually fuse lower down in the calice.

--- SENTENCE 3038 ---
The underside is rather flat and naked, except for the few scattered, subimmersed corallites mentioned above.

--- SENTENCE 3039 ---
The coenenchyma is scabrous and very echinulate.

--- SENTENCE 3040 ---
This species is in many ways similar to A. eurystoma (Klunzinger).

--- SENTENCE 3041 ---
It can easily be separated by its smaller axial corallites and much better developed septa.

--- SENTENCE 3042 ---
The latter characteristic is very striking, there always being two exceedingly well developed cycles of septa in every calice.

--- SENTENCE 3043 ---
Station.—Pago Pago Harbor, Tutuila, Samoa.

--- SENTENCE 3044 ---

#### Family PORITIDÆ Dana.

#### Genus PORITES Link.

1807.

--- SENTENCE 3045 ---
Porites Link, Beschreib, Natur.

--- SENTENCE 3046 ---
Rostock, p. 162.

1905.

--- SENTENCE 3047 ---
Porites Bernard, Porites of the Indo-Pacific Region, pp.

--- SENTENCE 3048 ---
303, 35 plates.

1918.

--- SENTENCE 3049 ---
Porites Vaughan, Carnegie Inst.

--- SENTENCE 3050 ---
Wash. Pub. 213, p. 188.

--- SENTENCE 3051 ---
Type species: Madrepora porites Pallas.

--- SENTENCE 3052 ---
Porites is without doubt one of the most difficult of coral genera.

--- SENTENCE 3053 ---
Bernard found it a nearly hopeless task to arrange the specimens of the British Museum according to species, and thus originated his locality-number scheme which he used in the above-mentioned publication.

--- SENTENCE 3054 ---
Vaughan, by following a carefully worked-out plan, which he outlines on page 189 of his Murray Island report, has succeeded admirably in applying a specific classification to the specimens he studied.

--- SENTENCE 3055 ---
The following papers deal with the general morphology of the poritid skeleton.

--- SENTENCE 3056 ---
On the structure of *Porites*, with preliminary notes on the soft parts.

--- SENTENCE 3057 ---
487-503, plate 35, 1900.

--- SENTENCE 3058 ---
*Porites* of the Indo-Pacific Region, Cat. of Madrepor.

--- SENTENCE 3059 ---
Mus. (Nat. Hist.), vol. 5, 1905.

--- SENTENCE 3060 ---
VAUGHAN, T. W. Recent Madreporaria of the Hawaiian Islands and Laysan.

--- SENTENCE 3061 ---
H. M. Bernard's work on the poritid corals (review).

--- SENTENCE 3062 ---
Science, n. s., vol. 26, pp. 373-378, Sept. 20, 1907.

--- SENTENCE 3063 ---
I am considering 8 species of *Porites* in this paper and have tried to follow as nearly as possible Vaughan's method of treatment.

#### Porites lobata Dana.

1846.

--- SENTENCE 3064 ---
Porites lobata Dana, U. S. Expl.

--- SENTENCE 3065 ---
Exp., Zooph., p. 562, plate 55, fig.

--- SENTENCE 3066 ---
Porites lobata Vaughan, Bull. 59, U. S. Nat.

--- SENTENCE 3067 ---
p. 196, plate 81, figs. 1, 1a, 1b; plates 82, 83; plate 84, figs.

--- SENTENCE 3068 ---
1, 1a, 1b, 2; plate 85, fig.

--- SENTENCE 3069 ---
Vaughan, in the above-mentioned paper, recognized six formæ of this species.

--- SENTENCE 3070 ---
He further divided one of these formæ, forma centralis, into 5 subformæ, which he designated as alpha, beta, gamma, delta, and epsilon.

--- SENTENCE 3071 ---
All of the original specimens of these formæ, including Dana's type specimen of the species, are in the U. S. National Museum.

--- SENTENCE 3072 ---
The necessity of this rather profuse division bears testimony of the enormous variation within the species.

--- SENTENCE 3073 ---
Although the formæ are distinct from each other, the general characteristics are decidedly similar enough to unite them into one species.

--- SENTENCE 3074 ---
In studying these specimens as classified by Dr. Vaughan, one can not help admiring the excellent manner in which he treated them.

--- SENTENCE 3075 ---
Following his plan, I wish to add another forma to the list, forma nodulosa, which is the Samoan collections representative of the species.

--- SENTENCE 3076 ---
#### Porites lobata forma nodulosa, new forma.

--- SENTENCE 3077 ---
The following is a description of one of the ten representatives of the forma:

--- SENTENCE 3078 ---
Corallum irregularly nodular, glomerate, rounded on all sides; dimensions, 6 cm.

--- SENTENCE 3079 ---
wide, 3.2 cm. thick.

--- SENTENCE 3080 ---
Calices polygonal, usually pentagonal, diameter of mature ones 1.4 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3081 ---
On upper surface calicular cavities are rather deep and thin-walled, but much shallower and slightly thicker-walled on under surface.

--- SENTENCE 3082 ---
On the upper surface there may be between the palus and the wall from I to 3 denticles (usually 2), which are moderately tall and spinulose.

--- SENTENCE 3083 ---
These denticles are caused by processes from one trabecula.

--- SENTENCE 3084 ---
The pali are perhaps slightly taller than the denticles and are very similar in appearance.

--- SENTENCE 3085 ---
The ring of synapticulæ joining the pali is rather deeply seated in the calice.

--- SENTENCE 3086 ---
The peripheral synapticular ring may or may not be complete.

--- SENTENCE 3087 ---
The trimurate structure is often present.

--- SENTENCE 3088 ---
The septal faces are rather roughly granulate.

--- SENTENCE 3089 ---
The columella is a compressed style with sharp echinulations, and at times reaches to nearly the height of the pali, but usually is much shorter.

--- SENTENCE 3090 ---
It is often joined by irregular radii to the septa.

--- SENTENCE 3091 ---
On the under surface the calices are shallower and the trimurate character of the walls much more noticeable because of the completeness of the peripheral ring of synapticulæ.

--- SENTENCE 3092 ---
The septa are much thicker and more roughly spinulose; the columella reaches to same height as pali and denticles.

--- SENTENCE 3093 ---
The calices, including the walls, give very much the appearance of a revolving wheel with thick spokes.

--- SENTENCE 3094 ---
This forma resembles in respect to the number of denticles the forma infundibulum of Vaughan.

--- SENTENCE 3095 ---
It does not have, however, the sloping margins of the septa, as does the latter forma.

--- SENTENCE 3096 ---
It is probably closer to forma centralis in general calicular arrangements than to any of the others.

--- SENTENCE 3097 ---
Stations, Pago Pago Harbor, Tutuila, Samoa:

Nos.

--- SENTENCE 3098 ---
Aua line, 700 to 724 feet from shore.

--- SENTENCE 3099 ---
Aua line, 766 to 790 feet from shore.

--- SENTENCE 3100 ---
Distribution of Porites lobata.—Hawaiian Islands; Fanning Island; Fiji Islands; Samoa Islands.

#### Porites lutea Milne Edwards and Haime.

--- SENTENCE 3101 ---
2a to 2c, 3. Also illustrated in Mayor's "Growth-Rate of Samoan Corals."

1846.

--- SENTENCE 3102 ---
Porites conglomerata Dana, U. S. Expl.

--- SENTENCE 3103 ---
Exp., Zooph., p. 561, plate 55, figs.

--- SENTENCE 3104 ---
Porites lutea Vaughan, Carnegie Inst.

--- SENTENCE 3105 ---
Wash. Pub. 213, p. 198, plate 88, figs.

--- SENTENCE 3106 ---
Porites haddoni Vaughan, Carnegie Inst.

--- SENTENCE 3107 ---
Wash. Pub. 213, p. 197, plate 87, figs.

--- SENTENCE 3108 ---
In the above-mentioned paper Dr. Vaughan discussed *Porites lutea* and placed the *P. fidjiensis secunda* and *P. fidjiensis decima* of Bernard in its synonymy.

--- SENTENCE 3109 ---
The Samoan collection contains a good-sized suite of specimens belonging to this species.

--- SENTENCE 3110 ---
The species is a very difficult one to deal with, because of the variation within it, and also because of the fact that the name *lutea* has been recklessly applied by many authors.

--- SENTENCE 3111 ---
For these reasons it has been difficult to ascertain exactly what *P. lutea* is really like.

--- SENTENCE 3112 ---
On page 244 of Bernard's Catalogue of *Porites* he states that in all probability Milne Edwards and Haime's description of *P. lutea* was based upon Dana's *P. conglomerata* (op.

--- SENTENCE 3113 ---
Dana's specimen of the latter species, which is No. 683, U. S. National Museum, is therefore, as Vaughan has observed (op.

--- SENTENCE 3114 ---
the type of *P. lutea*.

--- SENTENCE 3115 ---
Bernard's plates and description of *Porites fidjiensis secunda* 

corresponds to this type specimen very well.

--- SENTENCE 3116 ---
The great variation within the species occurs in the following features:

(1) The wall.—In typical P. lutea the wall consists of an irregular median ridge of fused trabeculæ, on each side of which there is a row of granules or flakes joined together by a rather thick synapticular ring.

--- SENTENCE 3117 ---
In this case the entire partition between the calicular openings appears rather thick and ragged.

--- SENTENCE 3118 ---
In some specimens, however, the walls are more delicately fashioned.

--- SENTENCE 3119 ---
They consist of a thin, raised, median ridge, within and below which is a slender ring of septal granules joined together by synapticulæ and separated from the median ridge by a ring of pores.

--- SENTENCE 3120 ---
These two types of walls may appear on the same colony.

--- SENTENCE 3121 ---
The median ridges often zigzag, but many times are perfectly straight.

--- SENTENCE 3122 ---
(2) The pali.—In different parts of the same specimen and in different specimens the pali may vary a good deal in shape and size.

--- SENTENCE 3123 ---
They may consist of slender, closely granulated, upright protuberances; they may be in the form of ridges compressed in the plane of the septa; they may be V-shaped above the laterals; or they may be very insignificant, pointed elevations.

--- SENTENCE 3124 ---
As a rule, the pali are distributed according to Bernard's illustration 3B, although occasionally they may be arranged

as in 3c.

--- SENTENCE 3125 ---
(3) Amount of granulation.—In places, especially at the basal portions of the corallum, the walls and septa may be thickly covered with flaky granulations, which produce rather a dense appearance.

--- SENTENCE 3126 ---
In other parts of the corallum they may be remarkably free of granulations.

--- SENTENCE 3127 ---
(4) The columella tubercle.—The columella tubercle may be well developed and prominent or very small and insignificant.

--- SENTENCE 3128 ---
Frequently it consists of a narrow, com-

pressed, granulated plate, but it is often styliform.

--- SENTENCE 3129 ---
(5) Depth of calicular depression.—Within certain limits the depth of the calices does not offer a specific difference because of the great variation in this respect.

--- SENTENCE 3130 ---
The growth experiments performed by Dr. Mayor demonstrate this fact.

--- SENTENCE 3131 ---
According to these experiments, deep, quiet water seems to promote a very smooth surface and shallow calices, while rough water appears to cause the deeper calices to occur.

--- SENTENCE 3132 ---
This is not a general truth, however, because several of the specimens in the collection which were grown in quiet water have calices of the normal depth.

--- SENTENCE 3133 ---
Vaughan described a new species, P. haddoni (op.

--- SENTENCE 3134 ---
cit., p. 197, plate 87, figs.

--- SENTENCE 3135 ---
1, 1a, 1b), and referred Bernard's P. Great Barrier Reef (42) 23 to its synonymy.

--- SENTENCE 3136 ---
Bernard's specimens show a palar formula equivalent to his figure 3c.

--- SENTENCE 3137 ---
In other words, in the trident there is one large palus above the point where the two laterals curve around and fuse with the ventral directive.

--- SENTENCE 3138 ---
In Vaughan's type of P. haddoni this is occasionally the case, but in the majority of calices the pali are arranged as

shown in Bernard's figure 3B, i. e., there are smaller pali above all three septa of the trident and the joining of the septa takes place farther down by means of the inner synapticular ring.

--- SENTENCE 3139 ---
There is, then, some doubt as to whether Bernard's specimens can be included in the synonymy of P. haddoni, although they seem to agree in all other respects.

--- SENTENCE 3140 ---
The palar arrangement, as illustrated by Bernard's figure 3B, is rather constant in all specimens of P. lutea that I have examined, including Dana's type of P. conglomerata.

--- SENTENCE 3141 ---
I have examined all the specimens of P. haddoni in the U. S. National Museum and find that they are so similar to P. lutea that I believe they represent a variety of the latter.

--- SENTENCE 3142 ---
The Samoan collection contains a number of specimens belonging to this variety.

--- SENTENCE 3143 ---
I am separating typical P. lutea from the haddoni variety by-

- (1) The walls.—Typical P. lutea has walls such as are described above, while the haddoni variety possesses the more delicately fashioned type, also previously mentioned.

--- SENTENCE 3144 ---
Around the basal parts of the corallum in the latter the corallite walls often appear exactly like those of typical lutea.

--- SENTENCE 3145 ---
As a rule, the walls of the latter are zigzag, while those of the variety are usually straight.

--- SENTENCE 3146 ---
This varies, however, in different parts of the corallum.

--- SENTENCE 3147 ---
- (2) The pali.—As a rule, I believe the pali are taller in the variety and can easily be distinguished by the naked eye.

--- SENTENCE 3148 ---

--- SENTENCE 3182 ---
#### Porites murrayensis Vaughan.

--- SENTENCE 3183 ---
Porites murrayensis Vaughan, Carnegie Inst.

--- SENTENCE 3184 ---
Wash. Pub. 213, p. 192, plate 84, figs.

--- SENTENCE 3185 ---
4, 4a, 4b, 5; also plate 13, figs. 12, 14.

--- SENTENCE 3186 ---
The specimens which Vaughan described in the above work show very little variation.

--- SENTENCE 3187 ---
He summarized the characteristics of the species as follows:

"Growth-form massive; calices with deep fossa, 1 to 1.5 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3188 ---
in diameter; wall elevated, interrupted in places; pali, the formula complete, or reduced on the directives and on the laterals of the triplet; columella tubercle frequently absent.'

--- SENTENCE 3189 ---
The Samoan specimens show a good deal of variation in different parts of the same colony and between different colonies.

--- SENTENCE 3190 ---
These variations appear in the following features:

- (1) Length of septa.—In the type specimens the septa are rather short and the axial fossæ, therefore, comparatively wide.

--- SENTENCE 3191 ---
This is true in the Samoan specimens in places, but in general they have slightly longer septa than the Murray Island ones.
- (2) Depth of columellar pit.—Usually there is a vertical drop of the inner septal margins into a deep columellar pit.

--- SENTENCE 3192 ---
On the undersides of some specimens the pit is not nearly so deep, and in a few calices a styliform columella may be exposed.

--- SENTENCE 3193 ---
This is true not only on the lower parts, but may apply to the entire corallum in the case of specimens which were subjected to adverse conditions.

--- SENTENCE 3194 ---
In normal specimens the columella is either poorly developed or entirely absent.

--- SENTENCE 3195 ---
(3) Thickness of wall.—On the underside of some specimens the wall becomes thickened, with a corresponding shrinkage of the calicular fossa.

--- SENTENCE 3196 ---
(4) Development of pali.—As in the type specimens, the pali are usually insignificant, but in places they may be prominent, especially those before the lateral pair.

--- SENTENCE 3197 ---
This is not restricted to the lower areas of the corallum, but may occur at any place on the surface.

--- SENTENCE 3198 ---
The majority of the Samoan specimens are massive, nodular forms, while some are incrusting lithothamnion and thus take on irregular rounded shapes, according to the object which they incrust.

--- SENTENCE 3199 ---
They agree with the type specimens very well in

general, with the exception of the above-mentioned features.

--- SENTENCE 3200 ---
As Dr. Vaughan pointed out, the species is very close to P. brighami Vaughan.

--- SENTENCE 3201 ---
I do not feel, however, that the Samoan specimens offer a bridge adequate enough to unite them.

--- SENTENCE 3202 ---
Plate 22, figure 1, of Bernard's Catalogue of Australian Porites illustrates a coral which appears to be close to this species; also plate 4, figure 1, (Fiji Island)

--- SENTENCE 3203 ---
Bernard looks like P. murrayensis.

--- SENTENCE 3204 ---

--- SENTENCE 3230 ---
### Porites andrewsi Vaughan.

--- SENTENCE 3231 ---
See also illustrations in Mayor's "Growth-Rate of Samoan Corals."

--- SENTENCE 3232 ---
Porites andrewsi Vaughan, Carnegie Inst.

--- SENTENCE 3233 ---
Wash. Pub. 213, p. 203, plate 14, fig.

--- SENTENCE 3234 ---
16, and plate 91, figs.

--- SENTENCE 3235 ---
1, 1a, 2, 2a.

--- SENTENCE 3236 ---
The Samoan collection contains a splendid suite of over 30 specimens.

--- SENTENCE 3237 ---
Variation is in general comparatively small and, as expressed by Dr. Vaughan, "consists mostly in the form of the colony, whether the branches are or are not interfused, and whether crooked or rather straight; and in the degree of development of the flaky reticulum and its associated denticles.

--- SENTENCE 3238 ---
"

Dichotomous branching is by far the most common; near the summits the branches usually divide into two short, blunt, divergent branchlets.

--- SENTENCE 3239 ---
Even in the case of the specimens whose branches are interfused this tendency to form short summit branchlets is apparent.

--- SENTENCE 3240 ---
Several colonies which were divided and one half placed in shallow, agitated water and the other half in deep water show little varia-

tion in growth-form.

--- SENTENCE 3241 ---
The wall and the peripheral synapticular ring with the outer denticles often form a flaky reticulum if thickened.

--- SENTENCE 3242 ---
In some specimens this reticulum is much more developed than in others, and as a rule is better developed on the basal parts of the corallum than above.

--- SENTENCE 3243 ---
Where the coenenchyma is wide the calices are rounder and more superficial.

--- SENTENCE 3244 ---
The halves of corals grown in deep water show a decidedly better developed flaky reticulum than the corresponding halves of shallow, agitated water habitat.

--- SENTENCE 3245 ---
In the latter examples the walls are more elevated and the calices less superficial.

--- SENTENCE 3246 ---
There is remarkably little variation in other calicular characters.

--- SENTENCE 3247 ---
The columella

may vary somewhat in size, but is normally distinct.

--- SENTENCE 3248 ---
One specimen does show a remarkable difference between the shallow-water and deep-water growths.

--- SENTENCE 3249 ---
Plate 19, figures 24Da and 24Db of Dr. Mayor's paper on the "Growth-Rate of Samoan Corals" show the specimen in 1919, when it was first placed in its deep-water habitat, and in 1920, after one year's stay there.

--- SENTENCE 3250 ---
In 1919 it had the appearance and characteristics of any ordinary specimen of *Porites* andrewsi; in 1920, when it was removed, it had changed considerably.

--- SENTENCE 3251 ---
The following is a description of it now:

Corallum arborescent, about 11 cm.

--- SENTENCE 3252 ---
high and 12 cm.

--- SENTENCE 3253 ---
Composed of twisted, subcylindrical branches which fuse 3 or 4 cm.

--- SENTENCE 3254 ---
from the top of the corallum and then separate again, giving off furcate, snake-like, irregularly inflated branchlets with blunt, rounded tips.

--- SENTENCE 3255 ---
The branches measure 14 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3256 ---
in diameter at the base; the branchlets near the summit are 6 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3257 ---
in diameter where not inflated and as much as 10 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3258 ---
On most branches living tissue was confined to only that portion of the corallum above the place where fusion occurs, or, in a few cases, it may extend to the basal portions.

--- SENTENCE 3259 ---
The irregularly twisted, snake-like branchlets are rather delicate.

--- SENTENCE 3260 ---
The surface is very smooth, the calices are not only superficial on the basal portions of the corallum, but are just as shallow near the ends of the branchlets.

--- SENTENCE 3261 ---
In all parts of the corallum the calices are uniformly placed with the subcircular calicular openings about 0.75

The septa are not at all clearly cut, being rather obscured by the septal granules and pali.

--- SENTENCE 3262 ---
There are 6 prominent, bushy pali surrounding a fairly distinct, granular columella.

--- SENTENCE 3263 ---
Often the columella can not be clearly seen, but usually it can be distinguished in the midst of the taller pali.

--- SENTENCE 3264 ---
Between the pali and the septal granules there is a comparatively deep depression.

--- SENTENCE 3265 ---
There appear to be at least 2 septal granules between this depression and the wall, but they are fused and with other granules extend out over the wall.

--- SENTENCE 3266 ---
The mural summit is indefinite.

--- SENTENCE 3267 ---
The outer synapticular ring is completely fused with the wall and thus adds considerably to the thickness of the latter.

--- SENTENCE 3268 ---
The mural trabeculæ end in irregularly shaped, rough, frosted denticles which are incompletely fused and give the summit of the wall a somewhat scabrous appearance.

--- SENTENCE 3269 ---
The roughly granulated columella springs from a dense platform made by the lateral

fusion of the radii which join with the septa.

--- SENTENCE 3270 ---
The Porites capricornis of Rehberg 1 is very similar to this deep-water specimen, and yet it is difficult to form a definite opinion from Rehberg's inadequate description.

--- SENTENCE 3271 ---
He says that some of the calices are slightly sunken, but that they are mostly superficial.

--- SENTENCE 3272 ---
I find them all to be superficial.

--- SENTENCE 3273 ---
He speaks of the columella as being hard to recognize.

--- SENTENCE 3274 ---
I find this so in parts of the corallum, but generally it can be seen fairly distinctly.

--- SENTENCE 3275 ---
The color is a very light tan and not black, as in his specimen.

--- SENTENCE 3276 ---
The remarkable growth-form, however, is exactly as Rehberg's figure shows.

--- SENTENCE 3277 ---
This is so distinctive that they in all probability belong to the same species.

--- SENTENCE 3278 ---
As Vaughan observed (op.

--- SENTENCE 3279 ---
cit., p. 205), P. andrewsi is very similar to P. cylindrica Dana.

--- SENTENCE 3280 ---
He separated them because the septal granules of the latter "are smaller, less regularly developed, and not arranged in conspicuous rings separated by circular depressions, as in P. andrewsi."

--- SENTENCE 3281 ---
The Samoan collection of P. andrewsi

contains no specimens which might serve to link the two species.

--- SENTENCE 3282 ---
Besides the differences mentioned by Vaughan, the calicular fossa of P.

andrewsi is slightly deeper than is the case in the other species.

--- SENTENCE 3283 ---
A branching form from Samoa was reported by Ortmann (Zool.

--- SENTENCE 3284 ---
Jahrb. 3, 1888, p. 157) and was compared to P. decipiens Brueggemann.

--- SENTENCE 3285 ---
Studer reported P. decipiens from the Samoa Islands (Zool.

--- SENTENCE 3286 ---
Syst. 14, 1901, p. 390.)

--- SENTENCE 3287 ---
Bernard's notes on P. decipiens can be found in his volume on the Indo-Pacific Porites, page 93, under Porites Caroline Islands (4) 1.

--- SENTENCE 3288 ---
I believe it to be quite possible that P. andrewsi belongs to the synonymy of P. decipiens, and still this question can not be decided until the type of the latter is redescribed.

--- SENTENCE 3289 ---
The growth-form and the size of the calices are similar.

--- SENTENCE 3290 ---
In both species the calices are shallow everywhere, but more superficial near the base than higher up.

--- SENTENCE 3291 ---
I believe that the difference in respect to the thickness of the septa and prominence of the pali and columella may be due to the fact that in all probability Brueggemann described only the calices near the tips of the branchlets.

--- SENTENCE 3292 ---

## Porites (Synaræa) horizontalata, new species.

--- SENTENCE 3352 ---
The corallum is thin, explanate, horizontal, with undulating surface.

--- SENTENCE 3353 ---
It possesses no large protuberances and is smooth, except in places where the walls rise up slightly to form low, convex ridges around the calices.

--- SENTENCE 3354 ---
At the rim it is less than 1 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3355 ---
thick; farther in it becomes 6 mm. thick.

--- SENTENCE 3356 ---
In each of the three examples the larger part of the corallum is free; but it is attached at one side to a dead specimen of the same species.

--- SENTENCE 3357 ---
The underside of this dead specimen shows no attachment scar.

--- SENTENCE 3358 ---
The calices are about 1 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3359 ---
in diameter and in general keep approximately the same distance apart (1 to 1.5 mm.)

--- SENTENCE 3360 ---
In nearly every case they are sunken between the walls, which rise gently into low, convex mounds or ridges of irregular shape.

--- SENTENCE 3361 ---
The ridges never exceed 2 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3362 ---
in height and generally they are about 1 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3363 ---
or less in this respect.

--- SENTENCE 3364 ---
The septa are 12 in number, with jagged edges.

--- SENTENCE 3365 ---
Often the same arrangement of the septa so frequently found in *Porites* can be made out.

--- SENTENCE 3366 ---
The two pairs of laterals on each side fuse together.

--- SENTENCE 3367 ---
Those of the triplet are less developed than the others.

--- SENTENCE 3368 ---
The pali are prominent, usually 6 in number, one of them smaller than the others.

--- SENTENCE 3369 ---
They are grouped around a little pin-hole fossa which often has a minute tubercle showing below the surface.

--- SENTENCE 3370 ---
Behind the pali are rough septal denticles of the same height, which pass out into the cœnenchyma.

--- SENTENCE 3371 ---
The latter is made up of flaky trabeculæ with jagged ends.

--- SENTENCE 3372 ---
The summits of the ridges and mounds are more loosely reticular than the slopes.

--- SENTENCE 3373 ---
In general the cœnenchyma has a rather substantial appearance.

--- SENTENCE 3374 ---
The under-surface is completely covered with a rather thick epitheca, which

shows lines of concentric growth.

--- SENTENCE 3375 ---
The species is very similar to the form described by Bernard as *Porites China Sea* (19) 3, (1905, Cat.

--- SENTENCE 3376 ---
Indo-Pacific *Porites*, p. 167, plate 25, fig. 9), but the former appears to be more solid and does not possess excrescences of 3 to 4 mm. in height.

--- SENTENCE 3377 ---
Stations, Pago Pago Harbor, Tutuila, Samoa:

No. 1.

--- SENTENCE 3378 ---
Dredged in about 18 fathoms off Utelei Reef.

--- SENTENCE 3379 ---
Associated with Leptoseris Brown when alive.

--- SENTENCE 3380 ---
Dredged in 8 to 16 fathoms north of Aua Reef.

--- SENTENCE 3381 ---
Dredged from a depth of 12 to 14 fathoms near buoy A; fairly hard bottom with some mud.

--- SENTENCE 3382 ---
Dull drab in color.

#### Porites (Synaræa) faustinoi, new species.

--- SENTENCE 3383 ---
Porites Australiæ Borealis tertia Bernard, Cat.

--- SENTENCE 3384 ---
Indo-Pacific Porites, p. 147, plate 22, fig.

--- SENTENCE 3385 ---
4; plate 35, fig.

--- SENTENCE 3386 ---
I recognize 5 specimens in the collection as belonging to Bernard's *Porites North Australia* (8) 3.

--- SENTENCE 3387 ---
They are all thin, explanate fragments and in no case are the parts present by which they were attached.

--- SENTENCE 3388 ---
The tallest process from the flat base is 2.5 cm.

--- SENTENCE 3389 ---
Plate 23, figures 1a, 1b, 1c, illustrate No. 1 and give all necessary data.

--- SENTENCE 3390 ---
Stations, Pago Pago Harbor, Tutuila, Samoa:

Nos.

--- SENTENCE 3391 ---
1 and 2. Obtained from 3.5 fathoms depth off Aua Reef patch, in very quiet water protected from breakers.

--- SENTENCE 3392 ---
Growing on edge of a crevice.

--- SENTENCE 3393 ---
Dredged from 5.5 fathoms off north end of Aua Reef.

--- SENTENCE 3394 ---
Brought up in the snapper from depth of 6 fathoms, 100 feet seaward from edge of fringing reef in the small bay just west of Fagaia Bay.

#### Family FAVOSITIDÆ Dana.

#### Genus ALVEOPORA Quoy and Gaimard.

#### Alveopora verrilliana Dana.

1872.

--- SENTENCE 3395 ---
Alveopora verrilliana Dana, Corals and Coral Islands, 1st ed., p. 77, with fig. 1907.

--- SENTENCE 3396 ---
Alveopora verrilliana Vaughan, Bull. 59, U. S. Nat.

--- SENTENCE 3397 ---
Mus., p. 217, plate 91, figs. 3, 3a.

--- SENTENCE 3398 ---
The type specimen, which has been described and figured by both Dana and Vaughan, is No. 327, U. S. National Museum.

--- SENTENCE 3399 ---
Dr. Mayor collected two good-sized representatives of the species.

--- SENTENCE 3400 ---
They differ from the type in having slightly thinner and more fragile corallite walls and septa.

--- SENTENCE 3401 ---
The corallites of the larger specimen vary from 1 to 2.5 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3402 ---
Those of the smaller one are of the same size as the type (1 to 1.2 mm.).

--- SENTENCE 3403 ---
Stations, Pago Pago Harbor, Tutuila, Samoa:

No. 1.

--- SENTENCE 3404 ---
Shallow tide-pools on seaward edge of Utelei Reef.

--- SENTENCE 3405 ---
Aua line, 526 to 550 feet from shore.

--- SENTENCE 3406 ---
Distribution.—Hawaiian Islands; Samoa.

#### Alveopora allingi, new species.

--- SENTENCE 3407 ---
The collection contains 4 specimens, all of which were dredged from depths of 100 feet or more.

--- SENTENCE 3408 ---
They are branching, with short, bulbous, top-heavy, irregular branches.

--- SENTENCE 3409 ---
No. 3 consists of a stem with two divergent branches.

--- SENTENCE 3410 ---
It measures 10 cm.

--- SENTENCE 3411 ---
in height and just above the basal portion the stem is 3 cm.

--- SENTENCE 3412 ---
The branches at their bases measure 2 to 2.5 cm.

--- SENTENCE 3413 ---
in diameter and then rapidly become thicker and knob-like toward the summit.

--- SENTENCE 3414 ---
One branch measures 4.5 cm.

--- SENTENCE 3415 ---
in length and 5 cm.

--- SENTENCE 3416 ---
at its widest portion across the top.

--- SENTENCE 3417 ---
The other branch is smaller, but proportioned in the same manner.

--- SENTENCE 3418 ---
The upper surfaces of the branches are rounded, with lobations.

--- SENTENCE 3419 ---
A thin epitheca covers the complete stem and rises halfway to the summit of the branches.

--- SENTENCE 3420 ---
The surface of the epitheca possesses irregularly placed, wavy, concentric striations.

--- SENTENCE 3421 ---
The calices are polygonal to subcircular.

--- SENTENCE 3422 ---
The fully grown ones average 5 mm. in their greater diameter and 4 mm. in their lesser.

--- SENTENCE 3423 ---
The septa are in two cycles; all are of the same size and meet in the center to form a columella.

--- SENTENCE 3424 ---
This columella is simply a tangle of the fine spiral threads of the septa and has a bird-nest appearance in the bottom of the calices.

--- SENTENCE 3425 ---
The latter are from 2 to 4 or 4.5 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3426 ---
The walls are rather thin and fragile, considering the large size of the calices.

--- SENTENCE 3427 ---
The pores in the walls are about 0.5 mm.

--- SENTENCE 3428 ---
in diameter and are arranged in regular longitudinal rows about 0.5 mm. apart.

Stations, Pago Pago Harbor, Tutuila, Samoa:

No. 1.

--- SENTENCE 3429 ---
Dredged from a depth of about 100 feet off Loa Loa.

No. 2.

--- SENTENCE 3430 ---
Dredged alive at depth of 17 fathoms on a muddy bottom between Oceanic S. S. Co. buoy and mooring-buoy A.

No. 3.

--- SENTENCE 3431 ---
Dredged from a depth of about 100 feet north of buoy A.

No. 4.

--- SENTENCE 3432 ---
Dredged alive from 12 to 14 fathoms on fairly hard bottom north of buoy A. Associated with *Leptoseris*.

#### Class HYDROZOA.

#### Order HYDROCORALLINÆ Moseley.

#### Family MILLEPORIDÆ L. Agassiz.

--- SENTENCE 3433 ---
#### Millepora alcicornis Linnæus.

1758.

--- SENTENCE 3434 ---
Millepora alcicornis Linné, Syst. nat.

--- SENTENCE 3435 ---
10, p. 791. 1846.

--- SENTENCE 3436 ---
Millepora alcicornis Dana, U. S. Expl.

--- SENTENCE 3437 ---
Exped., Zooph., p. 543.

--- SENTENCE 3438 ---
The one specimen in the collection agrees perfectly with Quelch's description and figures of *M. confertissima* (Report on Reef-Corals, Chal.

--- SENTENCE 3439 ---
Exped., p. 193, plate 7, figs.

--- SENTENCE 3440 ---
I believe the latter should be referred to *M. alcicornis*.

--- SENTENCE 3441 ---
The species is very like M. dichotoma Forskål.

--- SENTENCE 3442 ---
Speaking of the similarity of the two, Klunzinger (Die Korallenthiere des Rothen Meeres, third part, p. 86) says:

"Millepora alcicornis Linné aus dem Antillen ist sehr ähnlich, unterscheidet sich aber dadurch, dasz die Aeste von Strecke zu Strecke handflächenartig zusammenfliessen und dann fingerartig auseinandertreten, in der Weise eines Elengeweihes; auch sind die Endläppehen geründete und spitzer.

--- SENTENCE 3443 ---
"

Vaughan (Carnegie Inst.

--- SENTENCE 3444 ---
Wash. Pub. 213, p. 206, plate 93, fig.

--- SENTENCE 3445 ---
1) gives a good illustration of M. dichotoma.

--- SENTENCE 3446 ---
Station.—Pago Pago Harbor, Tutuila, Samoa.

--- SENTENCE 3447 ---
Shallow tide-pool on seaward edge of Utelei Reef.

#### Millepora truncata Dana.

See Mayor's "Growth-Rate of Samoan Corals" for illustrations.

1846.

--- SENTENCE 3448 ---
Millepora platyphyllia Dana, including β truncata Dana, U. S. Expl.

--- SENTENCE 3449 ---
Exped., Zooph., p. 548, plate 52, figs.

--- SENTENCE 3450 ---
5, 5a, 5b, 5c; plate 53, fig.

--- SENTENCE 3451 ---
Millepora truncata Vaughan, Carnegie Inst.

--- SENTENCE 3452 ---
Wash. Pub. 213, p. 207, plate 93, figs.

--- SENTENCE 3453 ---
It seems to me that all the specimens referred to M. platyphyllia belong to the same species.

--- SENTENCE 3454 ---
As Dana's M. platyphyllia differs from Ehrenberg's, I am placing all of the former under M. truncata.

--- SENTENCE 3455 ---
The Samoan collection contains 9 specimens which I am placing here; 5 of them are lobed as in typical truncata, while the others are small, young, and incrusting.

--- SENTENCE 3456 ---
I can see very little difference in the character of the pores.

--- SENTENCE 3457 ---
The species is similar to M. incrassata Dana, but differs from it by its smaller gastropores.

--- SENTENCE 3458 ---
Stations, Pago Pago Harbor, Tutuila, Samoa:

No. 1.

--- SENTENCE 3459 ---
Off outer edge of Utelei Reef, near Goat Island.

--- SENTENCE 3460 ---
44D and 40D (Mayor).

--- SENTENCE 3461 ---
Off Loa Loa, from depth of 2 fathoms, in agitated, pure water.

--- SENTENCE 3462 ---
Pure agitated water in shallow tide-pools near seaward edge of Aua Reef.

--- SENTENCE 3463 ---
No. 4 = No. 7 of the 600-foot station (Mayor).

--- SENTENCE 3464 ---
Growing naturally, on a rock 600 feet

from shore, on the Aua Reef flat, in water about 1 foot deep at lowest tide in region of fairly quiet water.

--- SENTENCE 3465 ---
Shallow tide-pools on seaward edge of Utelei Reef.

--- SENTENCE 3466 ---
Aua line, 526 to 550 feet from shore.

--- SENTENCE 3467 ---
Aua line, 600 to 624 feet from shore.

--- SENTENCE 3468 ---

--- SENTENCE 3597 ---
This index includes the names of the higher groups and of the families, general species, varieties, and formae of Madroperaria mentioned in this publication.

--- SENTENCE 3598 ---
The valid names are listed in remain type and the synonyms in italic.

--- SENTENCE 3599 ---
When a species name follows a genus name that is synonymous with another genus name, both the genus and species names of the combination are italicized, even though the species name may be valid.

--- SENTENCE 3600 ---
The heavy-faced type used in the figures referring to the pages indicates that the descriptions may be found on those pages.
